# Situations the interview is known to handle (2025)

Written by `node engine/dist/main.js interview-coverage --write` from the rows in `interview/2025/coverage/`; check-all fails when it is not current.
No model is called. Each row names a situation as the answers a filer in it gives. The check walks the built interview with those answers, in the interview's own order, and the row passes only when the walk ends where the row says.

How to read a path: the numbered answers are the row's own (or a named set's, marked), with the question's number in the walk. Every other question on the way was answered by the background return (the last section): the answer a filer with nothing to report gives to the same question. The background answers only the kinds of question it is itself asked; a question opened by the row's answers must be answered by the row. A refusal met on a background answer, or an expected value the background supplied, fails the row.

704 rows: 704 pass, 0 fail.

| `business.none` | lane 6a, step 1 (the off-ramp) | A person who did not work for themselves lists no business and is asked nothing about one. | The return is completed, with `$sc@person[taxpayer]` = none. Not asked on the way: `qbi.suspended`, `qbi.carryforward`, `qbi.reit_loss`. |
| `business.simple_age_settled_by_birth_year` | N8 (the SIMPLE catch-up age band settled by the year of birth) | A person born in 1964 with a one-person business and a SIMPLE IRA who put $20,000 of their own earnings into it is not asked their age band. | Asked next: `person[taxpayer].simple_employer`, “For this person (you, or your spouse on a joint return) — How much did you put into your SIMPLE IRA for 2025 as the employer’s contribution for yourself, counting money paid in up to the due date of this return?”. Not asked on the way: `person[taxpayer].simple_age_at_year_end`. |
| `business.simple_age_asked_born_1966` | N8 (a birth year that leaves the SIMPLE catch-up age band open) | A person born in 1966 with a one-person business and a SIMPLE IRA who put $20,000 of their own earnings into it is asked how old they were at the end of 2025. | Asked next: `person[taxpayer].simple_age_at_year_end`, “For this person (you, or your spouse on a joint return) — How old were you at the end of 2025?”. |

Alternative minimum tax is outside scope; prepare Form 6251. |

Only section 199A dividend income without loss carryovers is supported. |

Only section 199A dividend income without loss carryovers is supported. |

## Filers someone else can claim as a dependent

Rows: `interview/2025/coverage/dependent_filers.yaml`.

| Row | Design row | Situation | How the interview ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dep.w2_student` | scout-dependent-filers §4, typical filer (a student with one W-2, claimed by the parents) | A single student of 19 with one W-2 for $6,000, whom the parents can claim, completes the return. | The return is completed, with `sa.dependent` = Yes. |
| `dep.marketplace_refused` | scout-dependent-filers §1.2, f8962.dependent_filer | A single filer whom a parent can claim enters a Form 1095-A, and is told that someone who can be claimed cannot take the premium tax credit. | Refused at `f8962.dependent_filer`: Form 8962 for a filer someone else can claim as a dependent. Someone whom another person can claim as a dependent cannot take the premium tax credit. If that person claims you, they file Form 8962 for your coverage and you do not, so leave out your Form 1095-A. Otherwise this tool does not prepare Form 8962 for you. |
| `dep.qss_refused` | scout-dependent-filers §1.2, sa.dep_qss (amb.2025.sa_dep_qss) | A widowed parent whom someone else can claim as a dependent files as qualifying surviving spouse, for which the worksheet gives no standard deduction. | Refused at `sa.dep_qss`: Standard Deduction Worksheet for Dependents, line 3, for a qualifying surviving spouse. The Standard Deduction Worksheet for Dependents gives no amount for a qualifying surviving spouse whom someone else can claim as a dependent, so this tool does not figure that standard deduction. |

- `dep.marketplace_refused`: 1. `$person` = the entries `taxpayer` [set claimable_single] → 2. `f1040.status` = `single` (None of these: I was not married (single)) [set claimable_single] → 3. `household.ever_married` = No [set claimable_single] → (2 background answers) → 6. `sa.dependent` = Yes [set claimable_single] → (52 background answers) → 59. `$f1095a` = the entries `form_1`

## Children and other dependents

Rows: `interview/2025/coverage/dependents.yaml`.

| Row | Design row | Situation | How the interview ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dependents.ordinary_child` | lane 2a, the ordinary child (N4) | A young child of the filer's own, never married, with no money of their own, who lived with the filer all year, is asked none of the precise tests. | Asked next: `dependent[kid].citizen_ssn`, “Is this person a U.S. citizen with a social security number? (If the card has a restriction printed on it, such as “Not Valid for Employment”, answer No.)”. Not asked on the way: `dependent[kid].months`, `dependent[kid].kidnapped`, `dependent[kid].married`, `dependent[kid].joint_return`, `dependent[kid].self_support_view`, `dependent[kid].self_support`, `dependent[kid].divorced_rule`, `dependent[kid].other_nontaxpayer`. |
| `dependents.married_refused` | dependent.married (the married person rules), beneath dependent.ever_married | A 17-year-old child who was married at the end of 2025 is still refused at the married person rules. | Refused at `dependent[kid].married`: Married person rules. A married dependent is outside this tool; see Married person in the instructions. |
| `dependents.widowed_asks_joint_return` | dependent.joint_return, beneath dependent.ever_married | A child who was married before but not at the end of 2025 is asked the joint return test. | Asked next: `dependent[kid].joint_return`, “Is a joint tax return for 2025 being filed that includes this person, other than one filed only to get back tax that was withheld or paid in advance?”. |
| `dependents.divorced_rule_refused` | dependent.divorced_rule, beneath dependent.parents_apart | A child whose parents live apart, and whose custodial parent released the claim, is still refused at the rule for children of divorced or separated parents. | Refused at `dependent[kid].divorced_rule`: Children of divorced or separated parents. The rule for children of divorced or separated parents is outside this tool. |
| `dependents.parents_apart_no_release` | dependent.divorced_rule, beneath dependent.parents_apart | A child whose parents live apart, with no release of the claim, goes on to the next question. | Asked next: `dependent[kid].own_money`, “In 2025, did this person have money of their own (pay, benefits in their name, savings, a trust, a loan) or own the home they lived in?”. |
| `dependents.tiebreak_refused` | dependent.qc_tiebreak (qualifying child of more than one person) | A child who is the filer's qualifying child and whom another relative could also claim is still refused at the tie-breaker rules. | Refused at `dependent[kid].qc_tiebreak`: Qualifying child of more than one person (tie-breaker rules). This person may be the qualifying child of another person. The tie-breaker rules are outside this tool. |
| `dependents.kidnapped_refused` | dependent.kidnapped, beneath dependent.lived_all_year | A child presumed kidnapped who lived with the filer for 3 months is still refused at the kidnapped child rules. | Refused at `dependent[kid].kidnap_residency`: Kidnapped child (Pub. 501; Pub. 596 for the EIC). A child presumed kidnapped by someone who isn't a family member, who lived with you for fewer than 7 months of 2025 without counting the time missing, may count as living with you under rules this tool does not apply. |
| `dependents.part_year_asks_months` | dependent.months, beneath dependent.lived_all_year | A child who did not live with the filer all year is asked the months. | Asked next: `dependent[kid].months`, “For how many months of 2025 did this person live with you?”. |
| `dependents.own_money_clear` | dependent.self_support, beneath dependent.own_money and the plain answer | A teenager with pay of their own who clearly paid less than half of their own costs is not asked the full support test. | Asked next: `dependent[kid].citizen_ssn`, “Is this person a U.S. citizen with a social security number? (If the card has a restriction printed on it, such as “Not Valid for Employment”, answer No.)”. Not asked on the way: `dependent[kid].self_support`. |
| `dependents.own_money_unsure` | dependent.self_support, beneath dependent.own_money and the plain answer | A teenager with pay of their own, where it is close, is asked the full support test. | Asked next: `dependent[kid].self_support`, “Did this person provide over half of their own support for 2025?”. |
| `dependents.citizen_with_number` | dependent.citizen, dependent.tin, dependent.work_ssn, beneath dependent.citizen_ssn | A child who is a U.S. citizen with a social security number is asked nothing more about citizenship or the number. | Asked next: `person[taxpayer].tin`, “For this person (you, or your spouse on a joint return) — Do you have a social security number, or an ITIN (the tax number the IRS gives a person who cannot get a social security number)?”. Not asked on the way: `dependent[kid].citizen`, `dependent[kid].tin`, `dependent[kid].work_ssn`. |
| `dependents.not_citizen_with_number` | dependent.citizen, beneath dependent.citizen_ssn | A child who is not a U.S. citizen with a social security number is asked the citizenship test. | Asked next: `dependent[kid].citizen`, “In 2025, was this person a U.S. citizen, a U.S. national, or a resident of the United States for tax purposes (a “resident alien”)?”. |
| `dependents.no_number_refused` | dependent.claimed_no_tin (a dependent without a number) | A child who meets the tests but has no social security number, ITIN or ATIN is still refused. | Refused at `dependent[kid].claimed_no_tin`: Dependent without an SSN, ITIN or ATIN (Form 1040, Dependents, row (3)). A dependent child who was born and died in 2025 without an SSN, entered as "Died" on row (3) with a copy of the birth certificate, death certificate or hospital records, is outside this tool. |
| `dependents.parent_multiple_support_refused` | dependent.multiple_support | A parent supported by the filer together with others, none paying over half, is still refused at the multiple support agreement. | Refused at `dependent[kid].multiple_support`: Multiple support agreements. A person claimed under a multiple support agreement is outside this tool. |
| `dependents.parent_support_clear` | dependent.supported, beneath the plain answer | A parent with little income whom the filer clearly supports is not asked the full support test. | Asked next: `person[taxpayer].tin`, “For this person (you, or your spouse on a joint return) — Do you have a social security number, or an ITIN (the tax number the IRS gives a person who cannot get a social security number)?”. Not asked on the way: `dependent[kid].supported`. |
| `dependents.parent_support_unsure` | dependent.supported, beneath the plain answer | A parent whose support is close is asked the full support test. | Asked next: `dependent[kid].supported`, “Did you provide over half of this person's support in 2025?”. |
| `dependents.housemate_all_year` | dependent.qr_all_year, beneath dependent.lived_all_year | A partner who lived with the filer all year is not asked again whether they lived there all year. | Asked next: `dependent[kid].supported_view`, “Did you pay for more than half of this person's living costs in 2025?”. Not asked on the way: `dependent[kid].qr_all_year`. |
| `dependents.housemate_part_year` | dependent.qr_all_year, beneath dependent.lived_all_year | A partner who did not live with the filer for all of 2025 is asked whether the time away was temporary. | Asked next: `dependent[kid].qr_all_year`, “Was this person away from your home in 2025 only for a temporary reason, yours or theirs: illness, school, work, vacation, military service, juvenile detention, or a stay in a nursing home for constant medical care?”. |

(If the card has a restriction printed on it, such as “Not Valid for Employment”, answer No.)”. Not asked on the way: `dependent[kid].other_nontaxpayer`. |

This person may be the qualifying child of another person. The tie-breaker rules are outside this tool. |

## Education, student loans and home energy improvements

Rows: `interview/2025/coverage/education_energy.yaml`.

| Row | Design row | Situation | How the interview ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| `l8b.sli_none` | lane 8b notes, student loans (no loan) | A person paid no student loan interest in 2025. | The return is completed, with `sli.any` = No. Not asked on the way: `sli.interest`, `sli.unqualified_any`, `sli.assisted_any`. |
| `l8b.sli_plain` | lane 8b notes, student loans (the common case) | A person paid $1,200 of interest on ordinary student loans, none of it paid by a program or a 529 plan. | The return is completed, with `sli.interest` = $1,200.00. Not asked on the way: `sli.unqualified`, `sli.assisted`. |
| `l8b.sli_family_loan` | lane 8b notes, student loans (a loan that is not a qualified student loan) | A person paid student loan interest, part of it on a loan from a relative. | Asked next: `sli.unqualified`, “How much of the interest was on those loans?”. |
| `l8b.sli_repayment_program` | lane 8b notes, student loans (interest paid by a repayment program or from 529 earnings) | A person's student loan interest was partly paid by a state loan repayment program for health workers. | Asked next: `sli.assisted`, “How much of the interest was paid that way?”. |
| `l8b.edu_none` | lane 8b notes, education (no student) | Nobody on the return was a student in 2025. | The return is completed, with `f8863.paid` = No. Not asked on the way: `person[taxpayer].edu`, `f8863.ban`. |
| `l8b.edu_no_form` | lane 8b notes, education (no Form 1098-T and no exception) | A student whose school sent no Form 1098-T, where none of the exceptions applies. | The return is completed, with `person[taxpayer].edu_1098t` = No. Not asked on the way: `person[taxpayer].edu_inst`, `person[taxpayer].edu_line23`. |
| `l8b.edu_four_years_asked` | lane 8b notes, education (the student with a Form 1098-T goes on to the conditions of the credit) | A student with a Form 1098-T is asked next whether the credit was already claimed in four earlier years. | Asked next: `person[taxpayer].edu_line23`, “For this person (you, or your spouse on a joint return) — Has the American opportunity credit already been claimed for this person, by anyone, in four tax years before 2025?”. |
| `l8b.energy_none` | lane 8b notes, home energy (nothing installed) | A person had nothing installed at home in 2025 that saves or makes energy. | The return is completed, with `f5695.any` = No. Not asked on the way: `f5695.solar`, `f5695.heat_pump`, `f5695.business_use`, `f5695.carry`. |
| `l8b.energy_solar` | lane 8b notes, home energy (solar panels, the common Part I case) | A homeowner had solar panels installed in 2025 for $20,000. | The return is completed, with `f5695.line1` = $20,000.00. Not asked on the way: `f5695.line2`, `f5695.battery_cost`, `f5695.line17a`, `f5695.line21a`, `f5695.joint_occupancy`, `f5695.line12`. |
| `l8b.energy_heat_pump` | lane 8b notes, home energy (a heat pump, the common Part II case) | A homeowner had a heat pump installed in 2025 for $9,000 at the home she lives in. | The return is completed, with `f5695.line29a` = $9,000.00. Not asked on the way: `f5695.line22a`, `f5695.line23a_i`, `f5695.line24a`, `f5695.line25a`, `f5695.line17a`, `f5695.line1`, `f5695.construction_later`. |
| `l8b.energy_fuel_cell` | lane 8b notes, home energy (fuel cell property) | A fuel cell was installed at the main home in 2025. | Refused at `f5695.line7a`: Form 5695 lines 7b to 11 (qualified fuel cell property). The credit for qualified fuel cell property (its capacity limit and the joint occupancy rule) is outside this tool. |
| `l8b.energy_business_use` | lane 8b notes, home energy (an item used for business) | Solar panels were installed on a home that is partly rented out. | Refused at `f5695.business_use`: Form 5695 for an item used less than 80% for nonbusiness purposes. Allocating an item's cost between business and nonbusiness use is outside this tool. |
| `l8b.energy_construction_later` | lane 8b notes, home energy (a home under construction not yet lived in) | Solar panels went on a house being built that the owner first lived in in 2026. | Refused at `f5695.construction_later`: Form 5695 Part I for a constructed or reconstructed home whose original use did not begin in 2025. Costs of constructing or reconstructing a home are treated as paid when your original use of the home begins; a home first used in another year is outside this tool. |
| `energy_reconstruct_later` | BUG-0969 (Part II costs on a reconstructed home) | A heat pump was put in a house being rebuilt after a fire, which the owner first lived in again in 2026. | Refused at `f5695.reconstruct_later_if_part2`: Form 5695 Part II for a reconstructed home whose original use did not begin in 2025. Costs connected with reconstructing your home are treated as paid when your original use of the rebuilt home begins, and the energy efficient home improvement credit ends after 2025; such costs are outside this tool. |
| `l8b.energy_joint_occupancy` | lane 8b notes, home energy (a home shared with another occupant) | Insulation was put in a home the owner shares with a housemate. | Refused at `f5695.joint_occupancy`: Form 5695 Part II for joint occupants (line 32a). Allocating the credit limits among joint occupants by the amounts each paid, with the statement line 32a requires, is outside this tool. |
| `l8b.energy_new_home_insulation` | lane 8b notes, home energy (improvements in building a new main home) | Insulation was put in as part of building the owner's new main home. | Refused at `f5695.sec_a_new_home`: Form 5695 Part II, Section A, with improvements related to the construction of the main home (line 17e Yes). Separating improvements related to the construction of a home from those that are not is outside this tool. |
| `l8b.energy_new_home_heat_pump` | lane 8b notes, home energy (equipment in building a new home) | A heat pump was installed as part of building a new home. | Refused at `f5695.sec_b_new_home`: Form 5695 Part II, Section B, for property installed in connection with the construction of a new home. The energy efficient home improvement credit is only available for existing homes; separating such property from property for an existing home is outside this tool. |
| `l8b.energy_door_no_qmid` | lane 8b notes, home energy (a door without its manufacturer number) | One exterior door was installed for $500 and the owner has no manufacturer identification number for it. | Refused at `f5695.doors_no_qmid`: Form 5695 line 19 (an exterior door without a valid QMID). An exterior door installed in 2025 qualifies only with a valid QMID reported for it; obtain the QMID from the manufacturer, or do not claim that door. |

## Your home, taxes you paid, gifts to charity and medical bills

| Row | Design row | Situation | How the interview ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| `home.renter` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (the home parent; a No spares the mortgage and property tax amounts) | A person who rents, owns no real estate and has no loan on a boat or camper. | The return is completed, with `sa.home_owner` = No. Not asked on the way: `sa.mortgage_interest`, `sa.line5b`. |
| `home.owner_with_mortgage` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (what sa.line8a and sa.line5b asked of everyone) | A homeowner paid $12,000 of mortgage interest shown on Form 1098 and $4,000 of property tax. | The return is completed, with `sa.mortgage_interest` = $12,000.00, `sa.line5b` = $4,000.00. Not asked on the way: `sa.home_other`. |
| `home.owner_no_mortgage` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (an owner with no loan still reaches the property tax) | A person owns a home outright and paid $3,000 of property tax. | The return is completed, with `sa.line5b` = $3,000.00. Not asked on the way: `s3.mcc`. |
| `home.boat_loan` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (the guard of a wrong No to the home parent) | A person who owns no real estate pays interest on a loan secured by a houseboat they can live on. | The return is completed, with `sa.mortgage_interest` = $3,000.00. |
| `home.no_state_tax` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (the state income tax parent) | A person in a state with no income tax paid none and had nothing taken from pay for a state fund. | The return is completed, with `sa.state_tax_any` = No. Not asked on the way: `sa.line5a`, `sa.state_tax_other`. |
| `home.state_tax_withheld` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (what sa.line5a asked of everyone) | A wage earner had $2,500 of state income tax taken out of pay. | The return is completed, with `sa.line5a` = $2,500.00. Not asked on the way: `sa.state_tax_other`. |
| `home.state_fund_only` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (state tax paid other than from pay is its own question, asked of everyone) | A worker in Washington paid no income tax but had $600 taken from pay for the state workers' compensation fund. | The return is completed, with `sa.state_tax_other` = $600.00. Not asked on the way: `sa.line5a`. |
| `home.state_tax_estimates` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (the two parts of line 5a, withheld from pay and paid another way) | A person had $2,500 of state tax taken from pay and also made $1,000 of estimated state tax payments. | The return is completed, with `sa.line5a` = $2,500.00, `sa.state_tax_other` = $1,000.00. |
| `home.property_tax_with_charges` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (the rules of sa.line5b as a child: the part of the bill to leave out) | A homeowner paid $4,000 on the property tax bill, $300 of it a trash collection charge. | The return is completed, with `sa.property_tax_leave_out` = $300.00. |
| `home.car_tax` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (what sa.line5c asked of everyone) | A person paid a yearly tax of $120 based on the value of a car. | The return is completed, with `sa.line5c` = $120.00. |
| `home.no_car_tax` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (a No spares the amount) | A person paid no yearly tax based on the value of a car or other belongings. | The return is completed, with `sa.personal_property_tax_any` = No. Not asked on the way: `sa.line5c`. |
| `home.no_gifts` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (sa.gifts in one line; the carryover is its own question) | A person gave nothing to charity in 2025 and has no gift carried over from an earlier year. | The return is completed, with `sa.gift_carryover` = No. Not asked on the way: `sa.line11`, `$gift`, `sa.charity_limited`. |
| `home.gift_carryover_only` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (the guard of a wrong No to sa.gifts; today a Yes to sa.gifts and to sa.charity_limited) | A person gave nothing in 2025 but has a charitable contribution carried over from an earlier year. | Refused at `sa.gift_carryover_if_no_gifts`: Schedule A charitable limitation / carryover. Income-limited and carried-over charitable deductions are outside scope. |
| `home.gifts_of_money` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (what sa.gifts covered: money given) | A person gave $1,000 in money to charity, nothing else, and no limit applies. | The return is completed, with `sa.line11` = $1,000.00, `sa.gift_carryover` = No. |
| `home.gifts_limited` | provisional redraft, lane 7a (what sa.charity_limited covered) | A person gave to charity in 2025 and the deduction is limited by income. | Refused at `sa.charity_limited_if_gifts`: Schedule A charitable limitation / carryover. Income-limited and carried-over charitable deductions are outside scope. |
| `home.gifts_and_carryover` | BUG-0817 (the carryover is asked of every filer, so a filer who gave in 2025 and has a carryover is refused on it) | A person gave to charity in 2025, no income limit applies to those gifts, and a gift from an earlier year is still carried over. | Refused at `sa.gift_carryover_if_no_gifts`: Schedule A charitable limitation / carryover. Income-limited and carried-over charitable deductions are outside scope. |

| `home.gift_restricted_shares` | gift.public_securities (securities not free to be sold are not publicly traded; Section B) | A person gave listed shares worth $8,000 that they were not yet allowed to sell. | Refused at `gift[g].section_b`: Form 8283 Section B. A group of similar items over $5,000 requires Section B and a qualified appraisal; publicly traded securities are excepted. |

## Who the return is for, the household, and tax already paid

Rows: `interview/2025/coverage/household_payments.yaml`.

| Row | Design row | Situation | How the interview ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| `payments.none` | lane 1, payments (no tax paid in advance other than from wages) | A filer from whose payments no tax was taken other than from wages, and who sent no estimated payments, is asked for no amount. | The return is completed, with `f1040.withheld_other_any` = No, `f1040.estimated_any` = No. Not asked on the way: `f1040.line25b`, `f1040.line25c_other`, `f1040.line26`. |
| `payments.withheld_on_1099` | lane 1, payments (f1040.line25b, tax withheld on a Form 1099) | A filer whose pension payer took federal tax out is asked for the amount on the Forms 1099. | Asked next: `f1040.line25b`, “How much federal income tax was taken out, in all, on your (or, on a joint return, your spouse's) Forms 1099 (box 4), SSA-1099 (box 6) and RRB-1099 (box 10)?”. |
| `payments.withheld_on_w2g` | lane 1, payments (f1040.line25c_other, tax withheld on Form W-2G and the other papers) | A filer with tax taken out of gambling winnings and none on a Form 1099 is asked for the amount on Form W-2G. | Asked next: `f1040.line25c_other`, “How much federal income tax was taken out of gambling winnings (box 4 of Form W-2G), or is shown as withheld on a Schedule K-1 or on Form 1042-S, 8805 or 8288-A?”. |
| `payments.withheld_complete` | lane 1, payments (both amounts given) | A filer with tax withheld on a Form 1099 gives both amounts and the return is completed. | The return is completed, with `f1040.line25b` = $1,200.00. |
| `payments.estimated` | lane 1, payments (f1040.line26, estimated tax payments) | A filer who sent quarterly estimated payments is asked how much in all. | Asked next: `f1040.line26`, “How much estimated tax did you (or, on a joint return, your spouse) pay for 2025 in all, counting any overpayment applied to 2025?”. |
| `payments.estimated_complete` | lane 1, payments (estimated payments entered) | A filer who sent estimated payments gives the total and the return is completed. | The return is completed, with `f1040.line26` = $4,000.00. |
| `payments.estimated_amended_overpayment` | BUG-0897 (an overpayment from an amended 2024 return applied to 2025) | A filer whose only 2025 payment is an overpayment from an amended 2024 return, applied to 2025, gives that amount. | The return is completed, with `f1040.line26` = $1,200.00. |
| `payments.estimated_january_2026` | BUG-0897 (the fourth payment for 2025, sent in January 2026) | A filer whose only estimated payment for 2025 was sent in January 2026 gives that amount. | The return is completed, with `f1040.line26` = $1,500.00. |
| `household.single_divorced` | lane 1, marriage chain (single; married before, not at the end of 2025) | A divorced person files as single, says the marriage was over by December 31 and that no husband or wife died in 2025. | The return is completed, with `f1040.status` = `single` (None of these: I was not married (single)). |
| `household.hoh_divorced` | lane 1, marriage chain (head of household; divorced) | A divorced parent picks the line about paying for a home, says the marriage was over by December 31, and is asked about the cost of the home without being asked whether they count as unmarried. | Asked next: `household.home_cost`, “Did you pay more than half of what it cost to run your home in 2025?”. Not asked on the way: `household.considered_unmarried`. |
| `household.hoh_married_not_considered_unmarried` | lane 1, marriage chain (head of household; married and not considered unmarried) | A married person who lives with their husband or wife picks the line about paying for a home and is told its tests are not met. | Refused at `household.hoh_fails`: Head of household filing status. Head of household is checked but the tests are not met; you must be unmarried or considered unmarried, have paid over half the cost of keeping up your home, and have a qualifying person. |
| `household.couple_not_asked_ever_married` | lane 1, marriage chain (a couple is not asked whether they have ever been married) | A married couple filing together says they were married on December 31 and is not asked whether they have ever been married or whether a husband or wife died. | The return is completed, with `f1040.status` = `mfj` (Married, and filing together with my husband or wife on one return (married filing jointly)). Not asked on the way: `household.ever_married`, `household.spouse_died_this_year`. |
| `household.single_entitled_hoh` | T-304 item 1 (a filer entitled to head of household who takes "None of these") | A never-married parent takes the single line, lists their young child who lived with them all year, and paid more than half the cost of the home; they are told head of household fits. | Refused at `household.hoh_missed`: Head of household filing status fits you. You chose single, but your answers meet the head of household tests (you were unmarried, and you paid over half the cost of keeping up a home for a person who qualifies you). Head of household gives a lower tax than single on the same income. Go back and choose head of household. |
| `household.single_parent_home_not_paid` | T-304 item 1 (single with a child, not having paid over half the cost of the home) | A never-married parent takes the single line and lists their young child, but did not pay more than half the cost of the home; single is right, and the interview goes on past the head of household details. | Asked next: `household.credit_disallowed`, “Has the IRS ever reduced or refused your (or, on a joint return, your spouse's) child tax credit (including the additional one), credit for other dependents or earned income credit, except for a math or clerical error?”. Not asked on the way: `dependent[kid].absence_no_return`. |

## Interest, dividends, foreign tax and accounts outside the United States

Rows: `interview/2025/coverage/investments_foreign.yaml`.

| Row | Design row | Situation | How the interview ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| `foreign.none` | lane 5a, foreign parents (a filer with nothing outside the United States) | A person with no account, asset, trust or company outside the United States or in a U.S. territory. | The return is completed, with `sb.account_any` = No. Not asked on the way: `sb.fbar_required`, `sb.form8938`, `sb.line8`, `sb.form5471`. |
| `foreign.account_fbar` | old bullet 1 (a financial interest in an account located outside the United States) | A person kept a savings account in another country that held more than $10,000, so the foreign account report is due. | Refused at `sb.fbar_required_if_foreign`: FinCEN Form 114 (FBAR). Foreign-account reporting and the required country list are outside this tool. Prepare the separate FBAR and Schedule B disclosures. |
| `foreign.account_signature` | old bullet 2 (signature authority over an employer's or a relative's account) | A person can sign for an account of their employer in another country and has no money of their own abroad; the foreign account report is due. | Refused at `sb.fbar_required_if_foreign`: FinCEN Form 114 (FBAR). Foreign-account reporting and the required country list are outside this tool. Prepare the separate FBAR and Schedule B disclosures. |
| `foreign.account_small` | old opening ("count it whatever its value", "closed, emptied or sold during 2025") | A person had a small bank account in another country, closed in March 2025; no report or form is due for it. | The return is completed, with `sb.account_any` = Yes. Not asked on the way: `sb.line8`, `sb.form5471`. |
| `foreign.account_8938` | old bullets 1 and 5 (accounts are specified foreign financial assets) | A person holds accounts in another country worth more than the Form 8938 threshold. | Refused at `sb.form8938_if_foreign`: Form 8938. | `foreign.asset_8938` | old bullet 5 (securities of a non-US issuer held outside a US account, an interest in a foreign entity) | A person owns shares of a foreign company, held outside any U.S. brokerage account, worth more than the Form 8938 threshold. | Refused at `sb.form8938_if_foreign`: Form 8938. Prepare the required foreign financial asset disclosure; this tool does not prepare Form 8938. |
| `foreign.asset_fbar` | old bullets 1 and 4 (a cash-value policy of a foreign insurer; a balance with a business abroad) | A person has a cash-value life insurance policy from a foreign insurer, which they did not think of as an account; the foreign account report is due. | Refused at `sb.fbar_required_if_foreign`: FinCEN Form 114 (FBAR). Foreign-account reporting and the required country list are outside this tool. Prepare the separate FBAR and Schedule B disclosures. |
| `foreign.asset_loan` | old bullet 5 (a loan to a relative abroad; an heir of an estate abroad) | A person lent money to a cousin who lives abroad; the amount is below every filing threshold. | The return is completed, with `sb.asset_any` = Yes. Not asked on the way: `sb.line8`, `sb.form5471`. |
| `foreign.trust_distribution` | old bullets 7 and 8 (a distribution from, grantor of or transferor to a foreign trust) | A person received money in 2025 from a family trust run from another country. | Refused at `sb.line8_if_foreign`: Form 3520 / Form 3520-A. Review and prepare the required foreign-trust information returns; this path is outside scope. |
| `foreign.trust_beneficiary_only` | old bullet 7 ("or being a beneficiary of a foreign trust") | A person is named as a beneficiary of a foreign trust, received nothing from it in 2025, never gave to it, and has no form to file for it. | The return is completed, with `sb.trust_any` = Yes. Not asked on the way: `sb.form5471`. |
| `foreign.trust_account` | old bullet 3 (an account of a trust in which you have more than a 50% present beneficial interest) | A person has more than half of the present benefit of a foreign trust whose bank account held more than $10,000. | Refused at `sb.fbar_required_if_foreign`: FinCEN Form 114 (FBAR). Foreign-account reporting and the required country list are outside this tool. Prepare the separate FBAR and Schedule B disclosures. |
| `foreign.company_director` | old bullet 9 (an officer, director or manager of a foreign company) | A person is a director of their employer's subsidiary in another country. | Refused at `sb.form5471_if_foreign`: Form 5471. You may have to file Form 5471 for a foreign corporation; this tool does not prepare it. |
| `foreign.company_small_share` | old bullet 9 (10% or more counting family shares), answered Yes to the plain question and No to the precise one | A person and their brother each own 6% of a foreign company; a brother's shares are not counted, so the precise test is not met and nothing is due. | The return is completed, with `sb.company_any` = Yes. Not asked on the way: `sb.line8`. |
| `foreign.territory_trust` | old bullet 8 (a trust supervised by a court in Puerto Rico, Guam or another US territory counts as foreign) | A person set up a trust in Puerto Rico that a court there supervises. | Refused at `sb.line8_if_foreign`: Form 3520 / Form 3520-A. Review and prepare the required foreign-trust information returns; this path is outside scope. |
| `foreign.territory_company` | old bullet 9 (a company organized in Puerto Rico, Guam or another US territory) | A person is an officer of a company organized in Guam. | Refused at `sb.form5471_if_foreign`: Form 5471. You may have to file Form 5471 for a foreign corporation; this tool does not prepare it. |
| `foreign.territory_account` | old bullet 4 (an account with a financial institution organized in a US territory) | A person has a large account with a bank organized in Puerto Rico and must file Form 8938 for it. | Refused at `sb.form8938_if_foreign`: Form 8938. Prepare the required foreign financial asset disclosure; this tool does not prepare Form 8938. |

Not asked on the way: `sb.line8`, `sb.form5471`. |
| `foreign.line7a_not_asked` | Schedule B line 7a not asked of the typical filer (interest over $1,500 and nothing outside the United States) | A person was paid $2,000 of interest by a U.S. bank, all of it their own, owns no bonds and has nothing outside the United States. | The return is completed, with `sb.territory_any` = No. Not asked on the way: `sb.line7a`, `sb.fbar_required`, `sb.form8938`. |

| `dividends.owner_without_dividends` | lane 5a, dividends (the wider parent: an owner of shares that paid nothing) | A person owns shares of a company that paid no dividend in 2025 and has no Form 1099-DIV. | The return is completed, with `div.any` = Yes. Not asked on the way: `f1040.line3a`, `qbi.dividends`, `div.box2b`, `div.box2e`, `div.box3`, `div.box9`, `div.box10`, `div.box13`. |

| `dividends.liquidation` | old bullet 3 (liquidation distributions) | A person received a liquidation distribution in shares of another company, box 10 of a Form 1099-DIV, and no dividends. | Refused at `div.unsupported`: Form 1099-DIV special distribution treatment. | `dividends.return_of_capital_over_basis` | old bullet 3 (nondividend distributions, a return of capital) | A person received a return of capital, box 3 of a Form 1099-DIV, that was more than what the shares had cost. | Refused at `div.unsupported`: Form 1099-DIV special distribution treatment. Prepare the required section 897/liquidation treatment or reconcile return-of-capital basis and any excess gain. |
| `dividends.return_of_capital_no_form` | BUG-0830 (a distribution with no Form 1099-DIV is counted at div.other_boxes) | A shareholder of a small company was paid, with no Form 1099-DIV, a return of capital that was more than what the shares had cost. | Refused at `div.unsupported`: Form 1099-DIV special distribution treatment. Prepare the required section 897/liquidation treatment or reconcile return-of-capital basis and any excess gain. |
| `interest.bank_only` | lane 5a, interest (the common case: bank interest and nothing special) | A person was paid $300 of interest by a bank, all of it their own, and owns no bonds and made no loans. | The return is completed, with `sb.bonds_any` = No. Not asked on the way: `sb.bond_special`. |
| `interest.nominee` | old item 1 (nominee income) | A person's Form 1099-INT for a joint account with their mother shows $300 of interest, half of which is the mother's. | Refused at `sb.unsupported_if_income`: Schedule B special treatment / Form 8815. Prepare the applicable adjustment or exclusion; this tool supports unadjusted taxable interest and ordinary dividends. |
| `interest.seller_financed` | old items 2 to 9 (bond and loan adjustments; here seller-financed mortgage interest) | A person sold their house in 2023 and the buyer pays them interest on the price. | Refused at `sb.unsupported_if_income`: Schedule B special treatment / Form 8815. Prepare the applicable adjustment or exclusion; this tool supports unadjusted taxable interest and ordinary dividends. |
| `interest.treasury_plain` | lane 5a, interest (a Yes to the plain parent and a No beneath it) | A person holds Treasury notes bought at issue and held all year; nothing about the interest needs adjusting. | The return is completed, with `sb.bonds_any` = Yes. |
| `interest.none` | lane 5a, interest (no interest and no dividends) | A person had no interest and no dividends. | The return is completed, with `div.any` = No. Not asked on the way: `sb.nominee`, `sb.bonds_any`, `sb.bond_special`. |
| `dividends.private_activity_bonds` | old bullet 3 (exempt-interest dividends; here the part from private activity bonds, box 13) | A person's municipal bond fund reports specified private activity bond interest dividends in box 13 of Form 1099-DIV. | Refused at `div.amt`: Form 6251. Review private activity bond interest for the alternative minimum tax; Form 6251 is outside scope. |

## Jobs, tips and overtime

Rows: `interview/2025/coverage/jobs.yaml`.

| Row | Design row | Situation | How the interview ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| `jobs.tips_all_qualify` | lane 3, tipjob.qualified taken apart (every test met) | A server's tips were all for listed work, left freely by customers in money, and lawful. | The return is completed, with `person[taxpayer].tipjob[diner].tips` = $9,000.00. |
| `jobs.tips_work_not_listed` | lane 3, tipjob.qualified taken apart (the occupation is not on the list; today such a filer answers No to s1a.tips) | A person got tips at a job that is not on the list of tipped occupations; the return is completed without the deduction and nothing more is asked about the tips. | The return is completed, with `person[taxpayer].tipjob[diner].listed` = No. Not asked on the way: `person[taxpayer].tipjob[diner].other_work`, `person[taxpayer].tipjob[diner].charged`, `person[taxpayer].tipjob[diner].unlawful`, `person[taxpayer].tipjob[diner].tips`. |
| `jobs.tips_other_work` | lane 3, tipjob.qualified taken apart (two occupations for one employer, one not listed) | A person got tips from one employer for listed work and for other work that is not on the list. | Refused at `person[taxpayer].tipjob[diner].mixed`: Qualified tips from an employer where some tips were not qualified tips. Splitting one employer's reported tips between qualified and nonqualified tips is outside this tool. |
| `jobs.tips_service_charge` | lane 3, tipjob.qualified taken apart (not voluntary, negotiated, or not in cash) | A server's tips from one employer include an automatic gratuity the restaurant added to the bill. | Refused at `person[taxpayer].tipjob[diner].mixed`: Qualified tips from an employer where some tips were not qualified tips. Splitting one employer's reported tips between qualified and nonqualified tips is outside this tool. |
| `jobs.tips_unlawful_service` | lane 3, tipjob.qualified taken apart (a service that is a crime, prostitution or pornographic activity) | A bartender got tips while serving alcohol without the license the state requires. | Refused at `person[taxpayer].tipjob[diner].mixed`: Qualified tips from an employer where some tips were not qualified tips. Splitting one employer's reported tips between qualified and nonqualified tips is outside this tool. |
| `jobs.overtime_from_pay_stub` | lane 3, otjob.method by the paper (total pay at time-and-a-half) | An hourly worker covered by the federal overtime law reads the year's overtime pay, at time-and-a-half, from the last pay stub. | The return is completed, with `person[taxpayer].otjob[plant].method` = `total_time_and_half` (The total pay for overtime hours, paid at time-and-a-half). |
| `jobs.overtime_not_covered` | lane 3, otjob.flsa (an exempt employee) | A salaried manager exempt from the federal overtime law was paid overtime; the return is completed without the deduction. | The return is completed, with `person[taxpayer].otjob[plant].flsa` = No. Not asked on the way: `person[taxpayer].otjob[plant].method`, `person[taxpayer].otjob[plant].statement`. |
| `jobs.overtime_special_rule` | lane 3, otjob.special_rule (a firefighter's work period) | A firefighter's overtime is counted over a work period longer than a week. | Refused at `person[taxpayer].otjob[plant].refused`: Qualified overtime compensation by a reasonable-method estimate, at an overtime rate other than one and one-half or two times the regular rate, or under a special FLSA overtime rule. Figure the amount by the method that applies to you; this tool covers the employer-reported amount and methods 1 to 4 at time-and-a-half or double time. |
| `jobs.overtime_other_method` | lane 3, otjob.method (none of the five ways) | A worker's overtime includes daily overtime in weeks of 40 hours or fewer that cannot be separated out. | Refused at `person[taxpayer].otjob[plant].refused`: Qualified overtime compensation by a reasonable-method estimate, at an overtime rate other than one and one-half or two times the regular rate, or under a special FLSA overtime rule. Figure the amount by the method that applies to you; this tool covers the employer-reported amount and methods 1 to 4 at time-and-a-half or double time. |
| `jobs.railroad` | lane 3, person.rrta asked first in the jobs topic | A person worked for a railroad in 2025. | Refused at `person[taxpayer].rrta`: Schedule 3 line 11 (RRTA path). This stage handles Social Security only. Use the railroad retirement instructions; excess Tier 2 tax uses Form 843 rather than Form 1040. |
| `jobs.no_w2_no_w2_screen` | round two, the W-2 screen (s2.w2_marks and s1.nqdc_pension asked only when a W-2 is listed) | A person with a state tax refund and no W-2 is asked nothing about the boxes of a W-2. | Asked next: `refund.box8`, “Is box 8 checked on any of your (or, on a joint return, your spouse's) Forms 1099-G that show a refund, or was any refund of a state or local tax charged only on income from a business?”. Not asked on the way: `s2.w2_marks`, `s1.nqdc_pension`, `scope.w2_marks`, `scope.artist_official`. |

| `marketplace.premium_refunded` | l8a (f8962.premium_adjust item: a refunded premium) | A premium for a 2025 month of Marketplace coverage was refunded in 2025. | Refused at `f8962.premium_adjust`: Form 8962 with a Marketplace premium this tool does not adjust. Coverage that began after the first day of a month is not allowed a monthly credit amount for that month (except coverage effective on a birth, adoption, placement for adoption or foster care, or the effective date of a court order, which counts from the first day), and the enrollment premiums for a month are reduced by any premium amounts refunded in 2025. This tool takes Form 1095-A column A as reported and does not make either adjustment. |
| `marketplace.midmonth_start` | l8a (f8962.premium_adjust item: coverage began after the first day of a month) | Marketplace coverage began on the 15th of a month, not on a birth or adoption. | Refused at `f8962.premium_adjust`: Form 8962 with a Marketplace premium this tool does not adjust. Coverage that began after the first day of a month is not allowed a monthly credit amount for that month (except coverage effective on a birth, adoption, placement for adoption or foster care, or the effective date of a court order, which counts from the first day), and the enrollment premiums for a month are reduced by any premium amounts refunded in 2025. This tool takes Form 1095-A column A as reported and does not make either adjustment. |
| `marketplace.void_form` | l8a (f1095a.void, unchanged in the model) | The filer counted a Form 1095-A that is marked VOID. | Refused at `f8962.void_form`: Form 8962 using a VOID or replaced Form 1095-A. Enter the form the Marketplace says to use, and leave out a form marked VOID, an original that a corrected form replaces, and an original that a later VOID form supersedes. Their premiums, SLCSP amounts and advance payments must not be used on Form 8962. |
| `marketplace.same_all_year` | l8a (f1095a.uniform route: annual) | The form shows the same amounts in columns A and B in all twelve months and column B is right; the filer types three amounts, not thirty-six, and the return is completed. | The return is completed, with `f1095a[form_1].mo_a` = $500.00. Not asked on the way: `f1095a[form_1].b_fixed_same`, `f1095a[form_1].in_a_jan`, `f1095a[form_1].b_counted`. |
| `marketplace.months_differ` | l8a (f1095a.uniform route: monthly) | The form covers January to April only; the filer is asked the months and the return is completed. | The return is completed, with `f1095a[form_1].in_a_jan` = $500.00. Not asked on the way: `f1095a[form_1].b_fixed_same`, `f1095a[form_1].mo_a`. |
| `marketplace.column_b_blank_same` | l8a (f1095a.uniform route: column B blank, corrected the same) | Column B is blank all year (no advance payments were arranged) and the correct amount is the same every month; the annual route, as today. | The return is completed, with `f1095a[form_1].mo_b` = $600.00. Not asked on the way: `f1095a[form_1].in_a_jan`. |
| `marketplace.unreported_move` | l8a (f1095a.uniform route: column B wrong, corrected not the same) | The form looks the same all year but the filer moved without telling the Marketplace, so the correct column B is not the same every month; the monthly route, as today. | The return is completed, with `f1095a[form_1].in_b_jan` = $600.00. Not asked on the way: `f1095a[form_1].mo_a`. |
| `marketplace.no_advance_payments` | BUG-0634 (f1095a.no_aptc: no advance payments, derived from line 33, column C) | The form looks the same all year and column B is filled in, but no advance payments were made, so column B may be wrong; the filer is asked whether the correct column B is the same every month. | Asked next: `f1095a[form_1].b_fixed_same`, “On this Form 1095-A — Once column B is corrected, is the correct column B amount the same for every month, January to December, and the column A amount the same for every month too?”. Not asked on the way: `f1095a[form_1].mo_a`, `f1095a[form_1].in_a_jan`. |
| `marketplace.column_b_doubted` | BUG-0634 (f1095a.b_doubt: another reason to believe column B wrong) | The form looks the same all year and advance payments were made, but the Marketplace's tax tool gives a different column B for some month; the filer is asked whether the correct column B is the same every month, before the yearly amounts. | Asked next: `f1095a[form_1].b_fixed_same`, “On this Form 1095-A — Once column B is corrected, is the correct column B amount the same for every month, January to December, and the column A amount the same for every month too?”. Not asked on the way: `f1095a[form_1].c_total`, `f1095a[form_1].mo_a`. |
| `marketplace.printed_differs_corrected_same` | l8a (f1095a.uniform route: printed months differ, corrected the same) | Column B is printed for only some months, and the correct amount is the same in all twelve with column A the same too; the annual route, as today. | The return is completed, with `f1095a[form_1].mo_a` = $500.00. Not asked on the way: `f1095a[form_1].in_a_jan`. |
| `marketplace.married_this_year` | l8a (f8962.marriage, unchanged in the model) | A couple who married during 2025 file together, and one of them had Marketplace insurance before the first full month of marriage. | Refused at `f8962.marriage`: Form 8962 Part V, Alternative Calculation for Year of Marriage. ## Married filing separately

| `mfs.community_property` | scout-mfs 1.2 (community property state, refused) | A married person filing apart from their husband or wife lived in Texas in 2025, and is stopped at the community property question. | Refused at `household.mfs_community_property`: Community income on a married filing separately return (Form 8958; Pub. 555). A married person who files a separate return, where either spouse lived in or had a permanent home in a community property state, may have to report half of the community income of both spouses under state law; Form 8958 and the community property rules of Pub. 555 are outside this tool. |

Correct the filing status or marriage facts. A person married at the end of 2025 cannot file as single; filing jointly requires marriage at year end or the death of a spouse during 2025. The head of household and qualifying surviving spouse tests are checked separately. |

## Married filing separately, credits

Rows: `interview/2025/coverage/mfs_credits.yaml`.

| Row | Design row | Situation | How the interview ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mfs.no_education` | scout-mfs 4 (gated off on a separate return, N8) | A married person filing separately is never asked about education expenses, since a separate return takes no education credit. | The return is completed, with `f1040.status` = `mfs` (Married, and filing apart from my husband or wife (married filing separately)). Not asked on the way: `f8863.paid`. |
| `mfs.hsa_spouse` | scout-mfs 4 row 11 (D5, the narrowed Form 8889 refusal) | A married person filing separately, with family coverage, whose husband or wife has a health savings account of their own. | Refused at `person[taxpayer].f8889[hsa].mfs`: Form 8889 line 6 on a married filing separately return, with a spouse who has an HSA. When you had family coverage and your husband or wife has an HSA of their own, the family contribution limit is divided between you (equally unless you both agree otherwise), and this tool figures that division only on a joint return. |
| `mfs.hsa_spouse_none` | scout-mfs 4 row 11 (D5, the narrowed Form 8889 refusal) | A married person filing separately, with family coverage, whose husband or wife has no health savings account, goes on past the question. | Asked next: `person[taxpayer].x_hsa_prior_excess`, “For this person (you, or your spouse on a joint return) — Before 2025, was more put into your HSA than the limit for the year, with some of the extra still in the account at the start of 2025?”. |
| `mfs.dc_spouse_deemed` | scout-mfs 4 row 9 (the guard of f2441.mfs_spouse_earned) | A married person filing separately and living with their husband or wife has dependent care benefits from work, and the husband or wife was a full-time student. | Refused at `f2441.mfs_spouse_deemed_if_married`: Deemed earned income of a student or disabled spouse on a separate return (Form 2441, line 19). Your husband's or wife's earned income on line 19 is considered to be at least $250 or $500 a month for each month they were a full-time student or not able to care for themselves, which this tool does not figure. |
| `mfs.dc_spouse_earned` | scout-mfs 4 row 9 (the spouse's earned income on line 19) | A married person filing separately and living with their husband or wife has dependent care benefits from work; the husband or wife was not a student or disabled. | Asked next: `f2441.mfs_spouse_earned`, “How much did your husband or wife earn from work in 2025? Count wages and net earnings from self-employment, less any self-employment loss, but not the dependent care benefits in box 10 of their W-2s. Enter zero if the total is zero or less.”. |
| `mfs.ptc_exception` | scout-mfs 4 row 10 (D6, Form 8962 line A) | A married person filing separately with a Marketplace plan says they live apart from their husband or wife and cannot file jointly because of abuse; the three-year limit is asked next. | Asked next: `f8962.mfs_three_years`, “Did you take the premium tax credit on a separate return under this exception for each of 2022, 2023 and 2024?”. |

## The opening (who the return is for, and the filing status)

Rows: `interview/2025/coverage/opening.yaml`.

| Row | Design row | Situation | How the interview ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| `opening.couple` | C.1 row 1 (married, filing together) | A married couple puts both incomes on one return and is not asked for a filing status or whose entry is whose. | Recorded without being asked: `f1040.status` = `mfj` (Married, and filing together with my husband or wife on one return (married filing jointly)), `person[taxpayer].role` = `taxpayer`, `person[spouse].role` = `spouse`. Not asked on the way: `f1040.status`. |
| `opening.couple_spouse_died` | C.1 row 2 (filing together, spouse died in 2025) | A person whose husband or wife died during 2025 files one return for the two of them. | The return is completed, with `f1040.status` = `mfj` (Married, and filing together with my husband or wife on one return (married filing jointly)), `household.spouse_died_this_year` = Yes. |
| `opening.pair_not_married` | C.1 row 3 (filing together, not married, no death) | Two people who are not married to each other choose one return together. | Stopped as conflicting answers at `household.filing_status_conflict`: Filing status inconsistent with your marriage facts. Correct the filing status or marriage facts. A person married at the end of 2025 cannot file as single; filing jointly requires marriage at year end or the death of a spouse during 2025. The head of household and qualifying surviving spouse tests are checked separately. |
| `opening.separate` | C.1 row 4 (married, filing a separate return) | A married person files a return apart from their husband or wife. | The return is completed, with `f1040.status` = `mfs` (Married, and filing apart from my husband or wife (married filing separately)). |
| `opening.living_apart_parent` | C.1 row 5 (married, living apart, child at home; may be head of household) | A married parent who lives apart from their husband or wife picks the line about paying for a home, says they were married at the end of the year, and is asked whether they count as unmarried. | Asked next: `household.considered_unmarried`, “You said you were married on December 31, 2025. Does either of these fit you?”. |
| `opening.unmarried_with_child` | C.1 row 6 (unmarried with a child or supported relative) | A person who has never been married and pays for the home of their child picks the line about a home, and is asked about the cost of the home without being asked whether they were married or unmarried. | Asked next: `household.home_cost`, “Did you pay more than half of what it cost to run your home in 2025?”. Not asked on the way: `household.married_dec31`, `household.spouse_died_this_year`, `household.considered_unmarried`. |
| `opening.widowed_with_child` | C.1 row 7 (widowed in 2023 or 2024 with a child) | A person widowed in 2023 or 2024 whose child lives with them picks that line and is asked its five tests. | Asked next: `household.qss_eligible`, “Do all five of these tests apply to you?”. |
| `opening.single` | C.1 row 8 (unmarried, none of these) | A person who has never been married, with no child or supported relative, takes the last line and files as single after one question about marriage. | The return is completed, with `f1040.status` = `single` (None of these: I was not married (single)), `person[taxpayer].role` = `taxpayer`. Not asked on the way: `household.married_dec31`, `household.spouse_died_this_year`. |
| `opening.married_picks_single` | C.1 row 9 (married, picks "None of these") | A married person takes the line that says they were not married, then says they were married at the end of 2025. | Stopped as conflicting answers at `household.filing_status_conflict`: Filing status inconsistent with your marriage facts. Correct the filing status or marriage facts. A person married at the end of 2025 cannot file as single; filing jointly requires marriage at year end or the death of a spouse during 2025. The head of household and qualifying surviving spouse tests are checked separately. |
| `opening.widowed_this_year_alone` | C.1 row 10 (spouse died in 2025, filing alone) | A person whose husband or wife died during 2025 files for themselves alone as single. | Refused at `household.widowed_this_year`: Filing status after a spouse's death in 2025. Your spouse died in 2025, so you are considered married for the whole year for filing status; file a joint return with your deceased spouse (married filing jointly) or a separate return (married filing separately). |
| `opening.birth_year_after_tax_year` | DECISIONS.md item 2 (a birth year is range-checked; the upper bound) | A person types a year of birth after 2025. | Stopped as conflicting answers at `household.birth_year_bad`: Year of birth not possible. Correct the year of birth. A person on a return for 2025 was born in 2025 or earlier, and a year more than 125 years before 2025 is taken for a mistyped year. |
| `opening.birth_year_too_early` | DECISIONS.md item 2 (a birth year is range-checked; the lower bound) | A person types a year of birth more than 125 years before 2025. | Stopped as conflicting answers at `household.birth_year_bad`: Year of birth not possible. Correct the year of birth. A person on a return for 2025 was born in 2025 or earlier, and a year more than 125 years before 2025 is taken for a mistyped year. |
| `opening.birth_year_latest_accepted` | DECISIONS.md item 2 (the upper bound itself is accepted) | A person born in 2025 is not refused for the year of birth; the walk ends at the refusal of a filer under 16. | Refused at `household.filer_under16`: A filer under age 16 at the end of 2025. A return for a person under age 16 at the end of 2025 (you, or your spouse on a joint return) is outside this tool. The archived instructions state no such rule; this is an entry check the owner decided on, recorded in the ambiguity register (amb.2025.filer_under_16). |

| `pay.tips_deduction_with_4137` | scout-tail A.8 item 4 (decision deferred), residual refusal (Schedule 1-A line 4b with Form 4137) | A server with unreported tips also wants the deduction for qualified tips. | Refused at `s1a.f4137`: The deduction for qualified tips included on Form 4137 (Schedule 1-A, line 4b). | `pay.medicaid_agency_code_ii` | BUG-0904 fix (Medicaid waiver pay through an agency, line 1d and Schedule 1 line 8s) | A home-care agency paid a woman $24,000 under Medicaid to care for her mother, who lives with her, shown only in box 12 of the agency's W-2 with code II. | The return is completed, with `person[taxpayer].mw_not_box1` = $24,000.00. |
| `pay.medicaid_business` | scout-tail A.1, residual refusal (Medicaid waiver payments in a home-care business, Schedule C) | A person who runs a home-care business got Medicaid pay for a client living with her, on a Form 1099-NEC. | Refused at `person[taxpayer].mw_business_if_1099`: Medicaid waiver payments in a home-care business (Schedule C). | `pay.medicaid_1099_over` | lane x2-paynow2, inconsistent amounts (Form 1099 part larger than the pay not in box 1) | A carer enters more Medicaid pay on Form 1099 than all the pay that is not in box 1 of a W-2. | Refused at `person[taxpayer].mw_1099_over`: Medicaid waiver payments on Form 1099 more than the payments not in W-2 box 1. The Medicaid pay reported on Form 1099-MISC or 1099-NEC is part of the pay not in box 1 of a W-2, so it cannot be more. Check the two amounts. |

## Other Schedule 1 income (lane x2-s1o)

Rows: `interview/2025/coverage/s1_other.yaml`.

| Row | Design row | Situation | How the interview ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| `s1o.rec_property_tax` | scout-s1-other 3.1, Pub. 525 Worksheet 2 (the typical filer of the topic) | A retiree who itemized for 2024 got back $300 of 2024 property tax in 2025. | The return is completed, with `rec.property_tax` = $300.00. |
| `s1o.rec_medical` | scout-s1-other 3.1, Pub. 525 Example 34 (the 2024 medical deduction asked beneath the reimbursement) | A person who itemized for 2024 had $500 of 2024 medical bills repaid by insurance in 2025, of which $200 had been deducted. | The return is completed, with `rec.medical_deducted` = $200.00. |
| `s1o.rec_not_itemized` | scout-s1-other 3.1, Pub. 525 p. 24, Deductions not itemized | A person who took the standard deduction for 2024 got back some 2024 property tax in 2025. | The return is completed, with `refund.itemized` = No. |
| `s1o.rec_not_2024` | scout-s1-other 1.1, residual refusal rec.before_prior_year | A person was reimbursed in 2025 for a casualty loss deducted for 2023. | Refused at `rec.before_prior_year_if_any`: Pub. 525, Worksheet 2 (a recovery for an earlier year). Money paid back for something paid or deducted before 2024 is figured on a separate worksheet with that year's return; that is outside this tool. |
| `s1o.rec_nonitemized` | scout-s1-other 1.1, residual refusal rec.nonitemized | A person was repaid in 2025 a bad debt deducted on an earlier return. | Refused at `rec.nonitemized_if_any`: Pub. 525, Nonitemized Deduction Recoveries and Amounts Recovered for Credits. Money paid back for a deduction taken without itemizing (such as a bad debt) or for an item on which a credit was claimed is outside this tool. |
| `s1o.rec_sales_tax` | scout-s1-other 1.1, residual refusal rec.sales_tax (Worksheet 2 footnote 2) | A person who deducted sales tax for 2024 got a sales tax refund in 2025. | Refused at `rec.sales_tax_if_any`: Pub. 525, Worksheet 2, line 2 (a refund of general sales tax). A refund of general sales tax is limited by the sales tax and income tax you could have deducted for 2024; that is outside this tool. |
| `s1o.rec_amt_2024` | scout-s1-other 1.1, residual refusal at rec.line11 (refund.exc_tax, Pub. 525 Subject to AMT) | A person who owed alternative minimum tax for 2024 got back some 2024 property tax in 2025. | Refused at `refund.exc_tax`: Pub. 525, Itemized Deduction Recoveries (Unused tax credits, Subject to AMT, Capital gains). Your 2024 alternative minimum tax, unused credits, 0% capital gain rate or sales tax choice means the 2024 tax is refigured to find how much of what was paid back is taxable; that is outside this tool. |
| `s1o.nol_computed` | scout-s1-other 3.3, the NOL deduction where the 80% limit cannot bind | A person earning $80,000 carries into 2025 a $20,000 loss from a business closed in 2023. | The return is completed, with `s1.nol_amount` = $20,000.00. |
| `s1o.nol_pre2018` | scout-s1-other 1.1, residual refusal s1.nol_before_limit_date | A person carries into 2025 part of a loss from 2016. | Refused at `s1.nol_before_limit_date_if_nol`: Form 172, NOL deduction limitation (a loss from a year before 2018). A net operating loss from a tax year that began before 2018 is deducted ahead of the limit on later losses; that is outside this tool. |
| `s1o.nol_ebl` | scout-s1-other 1.1, residual refusal s1.nol_ebl | A person carries into 2025 a loss held back in 2024 by the limit on excess business losses. | Refused at `s1.nol_ebl_if_nol`: Form 172 and Form 8995 (a loss carried from an excess business loss). A net operating loss from an excess business loss is taken into the qualified business income of the year it is deducted; that is outside this tool. |
| `s1o.nol_was_joint` | scout-s1-other 3.3, residual refusal s1.nol_marital (i172 Change in Marital Status) | A divorced person carries into 2025 a loss from a year of a joint return. | Refused at `s1.nol_marital`: Form 172, Change in Marital Status. A net operating loss from a year when you were not married to your spouse of 2025, or when you filed jointly and now file alone, must be divided between the spouses; that is outside this tool. |
| `s1o.nol_limit` | scout-s1-other 3.3, residual refusal s1.nol_limit at Form 1040 line 15 | A person earning $40,000 carries into 2025 a $30,000 loss, more than four times taxable income after it. | Refused at `s1.nol_limit`: Form 172, NOL deduction limitation (the 80% limit). Your net operating loss carried into 2025 is more than four times your taxable income after deducting it, so the limit of 80% of taxable income figured without the deduction may apply; figuring the limit and the loss carried to 2026 is outside this tool. |
| `s1o.ltc_per_diem` | scout-s1-other 3.4, Form 8853 Section C computed | A person was paid $160,600 at a set daily rate in 2025 by a long-term care policy on their mother, for care costing $120,000. | The return is completed, with `f8853c[mom].line17` = $160,600.00. |
| `s1o.ltc_reimbursed` | scout-s1-other 3.4, payments that only repaid costs (nothing taxable, nothing more asked) | A long-term care policy repaid a person's nursing home bills in 2025. | The return is completed, with `f8853c[mom].periodic` = No. |
| `s1o.ltc_others_paid` | scout-s1-other 1.1, residual refusal f8853c.other_payees (Multiple Payees) | A person and their brother were each paid at a set rate under policies on their mother. | Refused at `f8853c[mom].others_paid_if_periodic`: Form 8853, Section C, Multiple Payees. When someone else also received payments at a set rate for the same insured person, the $420-a-day limit is figured for everyone together on an attached statement; that is outside this tool. |
| `s1o.ltc_rate_changed` | scout-s1-other 3.4, residual refusal f8853c.rate_changed (more than one LTC period) | A policy's daily rate rose partway through 2025. | Refused at `f8853c[mom].rate_changed_if_periodic`: Form 8853, Section C (more than one LTC period). Payments at more than one rate in 2025 make more than one long-term care period, each figured on its own Section C; that is outside this tool. |
| `s1o.ltc_nonqualified` | scout-s1-other 1.1, residual refusal f8853c.nonqualified (line 17 caution) | A person was paid at a set rate under a long-term care policy that is not a qualified contract. | Refused at `f8853c[mom].nonqualified_if_periodic`: Form 8853, Section C, line 17 (a contract that is not a qualified LTC insurance contract). Payments at a set rate under a contract that is not a qualified long-term care insurance contract are taxable only to the extent they are not excludable, figured outside Form 8853; that is outside this tool. |
| `s1o.ltc_reimbursed_to_provider` | BUG-0873 (line 24 counts reimbursements paid straight to the care provider) | A person was paid $160,600 at a set daily rate for their mother's care costing $120,000, and a second policy paid $30,000 of the nursing home's bills straight to the home. | The return is completed, with `f8853c[mom].line24` = $30,000.00. |
| `s1o.ltc_others_accelerated` | BUG-0873 (line 15, an accelerated death benefit paid to someone else) | A person was paid at a set rate under a policy on their mother, and their brother was paid a life insurance benefit early because she was ill. | Refused at `f8853c[mom].others_paid_if_periodic`: Form 8853, Section C, Multiple Payees. When someone else also received payments at a set rate for the same insured person, the $420-a-day limit is figured for everyone together on an attached statement; that is outside this tool. |
| `s1o.ltc_weekly_per_diem` | BUG-0873 (a fixed amount per week is a per diem payment) | A person was paid a fixed $3,080 a week in 2025 by a long-term care policy on their mother, for care costing $120,000. | The return is completed, with `f8853c[mom].line17` = $160,600.00. |
| `s1o.inmate_amount` | scout-s1-other 3.5, line 8u, refused by s1.line8_other (computing it waits for the W-2 part of the pay to be asked; see TASKS.md) | A person was paid $800 in 2025 for work done while in prison. | Refused at `s1.line8_other`: Schedule 1, line 8 (other income this tool does not compute). Other income on Schedule 1 line 8 other than gambling winnings, canceled debt, income from Form 8889, Alaska Permanent Fund dividends, jury duty pay, prizes and awards, income from an activity not engaged in for profit, scholarship and fellowship grants not on Form W-2 and the supported line 8z items is outside this tool. |
| `s1o.fiduciary_fees` | scout-s1-other 3.2, a new line 8z kind computed | A person was paid $2,500 for acting as executor of a friend's estate. | The return is completed, with `oi8z[fees].amount` = $2,500.00. |
| `s1o.damages_injury` | scout-s1-other 3.2, residual refusal oi8z.injury | A person received a settlement, partly punitive damages, in a case about a car accident injury. | Refused at `oi8z[award].injury_if_damages`: Pub. 525, Court awards and damages (Schedule 1, line 8z). Damages or a settlement paid in a case about a physical injury or physical sickness are split between the compensatory part, which is not income, and any punitive damages, which are; that is outside this tool. |

## Sales of investments, loss carryovers and borrowing to invest

| `sales.installment_sale` | MAP 2.8, sd.other_forms (Form 6252) | A person sold land in 2023 and is paid for it over ten years, reporting the gain as the payments arrive. | Refused at `sd.other_forms`: Schedule D additional source forms. Prepare the required source forms; their gains/losses are outside the supported securities slice. |
